) commutes.

The proofs that
\lambda_{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}
satisfies the remaining three distributive law axioms follow similarly (and are somewhat simpler than the proof that (
6.23
) commutes.

Proposition 6.25
.

The monad
\mathbb{T}
on
\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{V}}
extends to a monad
\mathbb{T}_{*}=(T_{*},\mu^{\mathbb{T}_{*}},\eta^{\mathbb{T}_{*}})
on
{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{\mathsf{V}}
such that
{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{\mathsf{V}}^{\mathbb{T}_{*}}\cong\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{V}}^{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}
.

Proof.

For
\mathsf{V}=\mathsf{Set}
,
\mathbb{T}_{*}
has been described in detail in
[
41
, Section 7]
.

For graphical species in a general category
\mathsf{V}
with sufficient (co)limits, the endofunctor
T_{*}
is the quotient of
T
given, for all pointed graphical species
S_{*}=(S,\epsilon,o)
in
\mathsf{V}
, by

(6.26)

T_{*}S_{\lx@sectionsign}=S_{\lx@sectionsign},\text{ and }T_{*}S_{X}=\mathrm{colim}_{\mathcal{X}\in{X}\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{CGr}_{\mathsf{sim}}}S(\mathcal{X}).

The unit
\epsilon^{T_{*}S}\overset{\textup{def}}{=}T_{*}(\epsilon)\colon S_{\lx@sectionsign}\to T_{*}S_{\mathbf{2}}
is described by the obvious composite

\textstyle{S_{\lx@sectionsign}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\epsilon}
\textstyle{S_{\mathbf{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta^{\mathbb{T}}S}
\textstyle{TS_{\mathbf{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T_{*}S_{\mathbf{2}},}

and the contracted unit
o^{T_{*}S}\overset{\textup{def}}{=}T_{*}(o)\colon S_{\lx@sectionsign}\to T_{*}S_{\mathbf{0}}
is described by

\textstyle{S_{\lx@sectionsign}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{o}
\textstyle{S_{\mathbf{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta^{\mathbb{T}}S}
\textstyle{TS_{\mathbf{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T_{*}S_{\mathbf{0}}.}

In particular,
\epsilon^{T_{*}S}
is defined by maps
S(u^{k})\colon S_{\lx@sectionsign}\to S(\mathcal{L}^{k})
(for
k\geq 1
), and
o^{T_{*}S}
by maps
S(\kappa^{m})\colon S_{\lx@sectionsign}\to S(\mathcal{W}^{m})
,
m\geq 1
and
S(o)\colon S_{\lx@sectionsign}\to S(\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{0}})
such that
o\circ S_{\tau}=o
.

The multiplication
\mu^{\mathbb{T}_{*}}
for
\mathbb{T}_{*}
is the one induced by
\mu^{\mathbb{T}}
, that forgets the pair
(\mathcal{X},\mathbf{\Gamma})
– of an
X
-graph
\mathcal{X}
, and a nondegenerate
\mathcal{X}
-shaped graph of connected graphs
\mathbf{\Gamma}
– and replaces it with the colimit
\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{X})
. The unit
\eta^{\mathbb{T}_{*}}
for
\mathbb{T}_{*}
is defined by the maps
S_{X}=S(\mathcal{C}_{X})\mapsto\mathrm{colim}_{\mathcal{X}\in{X}\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{CGr}_{\mathsf{sim}}}S(\mathcal{X})
.

Let
(A_{*},h_{*})
be a
\mathbb{T}_{*}
algebra in
{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{\mathsf{V}}
such that
A_{*}=(A,\epsilon,o)
. The unique map
h\colon DTA\to A
that preserves distinguished (contracted) units and factors through the quotient
TA\twoheadrightarrow T_{*}A
on
TA
describes a
\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}
algebra structure on
A
.

Conversely, if
(A,h)
is a
\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}
algebra, then in particular,
A
has a
\mathbb{D}
-algebra structure
(A,\epsilon,o)
and
h
, together with
\lambda_{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}
, induce a
\mathbb{T}_{*}
algebra structure on
(A,\epsilon,o)
. (See the proof of
[
41
, Proposition 7.39]
).

It is straightforward (though somewhat laborious) to use the definition of
\lambda_{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}
– as in the proof of
[
41
, Proposition 7.39]
– to check that these assignments extend to inverse functors
{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{\mathsf{V}}^{\mathbb{T}_{*}}\leftrightarrows\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{V}}^{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}
.
∎

It is then straightforward to derive the following theorem (that, in the case
\mathsf{V}=\mathsf{Set}
, was the main result, Theorem 7.46, of
[
41
]
) for general
\mathsf{V}
.

Theorem 6.27
.

The EM category
\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{V}}^{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}
of algebras for
\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}
is canonically isomorphic to
{\bm{\mathfrak{MO}}}_{\mathsf{V}}
.

Proof.

Let
(A,h)
be a
\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}
-algebra in
\mathsf{V}
. Then
h
induces a
\mathbb{T}
-algebra structure
h^{\mathbb{T}}\colon TA\to A
by restriction, and a
\mathbb{D}
-algebra structure
h^{\mathbb{D}}=h\circ D\eta^{\mathbb{T}}A\colon DA\to A
. In particular,
h^{\mathbb{T}}
equips
A
with the a multiplication
\diamond
and contraction
\zeta
such that
(A,\diamond,\zeta)
is a nonunital modular operad in
\mathsf{V}
, and
h^{\mathbb{D}}
, equips
A
with the structure of a pointed graphical species
A_{*}=(A,\epsilon,o)
in
\mathsf{V}
.

To prove that
(A,h)
describes a modular operad, it remains to show that
\epsilon
is a unit for
\diamond.
But, this is immediate from the definition of the distributive law
\lambda_{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}
or, equivalently, from
Proposition

6.25
and (
6.26
).

Conversely, if
(S,\diamond,\zeta,\epsilon)
is a modular operad in
\mathsf{V}
, then
(S,\diamond,\zeta)
defines a
\mathbb{T}
-algebra
p^{\mathbb{T}}\colon TS\to S
by
Proposition

6.2
, and
(S,\epsilon,\zeta\epsilon)
defines a
\mathbb{D}
-algebra by
Section

6.2
. In particular, since
\epsilon
is a unit for
\diamond
, and
p^{\mathbb{T}}
is induced by iterations of
\diamond
and
\zeta
,
p^{\mathbb{T}}
induces a
\mathbb{T}_{*}
-algebra structure on
(S,\epsilon,\zeta\epsilon)
by (
6.26
). Hence,
(S,\diamond,\zeta,\epsilon)
describes an algebra for
\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}
.
∎

Example 6.28
.

(See also Examples
3.7
,
3.32
and
5.19
, and
Definition

3.33
.) The results of this section may be modified for oriented graphical species (see
Definition

3.8
). By restricting to connected directed graphs, we may obtain an oriented version of
\mathbb{T}
as in
Example

5.19
.
Since there are no orientation preserving automorphisms of the directed exceptional edge
\downarrow
in
\mathsf{OGr}
, the endofunctor
\mathsf{O}D
underlying the monad
\mathsf{\mathbb{}}D
on
{\mathsf{OGS}_{\mathsf{V}}}
such that
{\mathsf{OGS}_{\mathsf{V}}}^{\mathsf{\mathbb{}}D}\simeq\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{V}}^{\mathbb{D}}/Di^{\mathsf{V}}
is defined, for all oriented graphical species
\tilde{S}\colon{\mathsf{el}{(Di)}}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathsf{V}
, by
\mathsf{O}D\tilde{S}(\downarrow)=\tilde{S}(\downarrow)
and

\mathsf{O}D\tilde{S}(X_{{\mathrm{in}}},X_{{\mathrm{out}}})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\tilde{S}(X_{{\mathrm{in}}},X_{{\mathrm{out}}})&\text{ for }({X_{{\mathrm{in}}},X_{{\mathrm{out}}}})\not\cong({\mathbf{0},\mathbf{0}}),\text{ and }({X_{{\mathrm{in}}},X_{{\mathrm{out}}}})\not\cong(\mathbf{1},\mathbf{1}),\\
\tilde{S}(\mathbf{1},\mathbf{1})\amalg\tilde{S}(\downarrow)&\text{ for }({X_{{\mathrm{in}}},X_{{\mathrm{out}}}})\cong(\mathbf{1},\mathbf{1}),\\
\tilde{S}(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{0})\amalg\tilde{S}(\downarrow)&\text{ for }({X_{{\mathrm{in}}},X_{{\mathrm{out}}}})\cong(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{0})\\
\end{array}\right.

A category
\mathsf{OGr_{*}}
of pointed directed graphs and a distributive law are obtained exactly as for the undirected case. This is equipped with distinguished morphisms
\hat{z}\colon\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{0}}\to(\downarrow)
and
\hat{u}\colon\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{1},\mathbf{1}}\to(\downarrow)
satisfying

\hat{u}\circ i_{1}=\hat{u}\circ o_{1}=id_{\downarrow}
.

Algebras for the composite monad on
{\mathsf{OGS}_{\mathsf{V}}}
so-obtained are wheeled properads in
\mathsf{V}
.

Remark 6.29
.

By
Remark

3.34
, circuit algebras are, in particular monoid objects in
\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{V}}
. However, they do not describe monoids in the category
{\bm{\mathfrak{MO}}}_{\mathsf{V}}
of modular operads since
{\bm{\mathfrak{MO}}}_{\mathsf{V}}\simeq\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{V}}^{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}
is not closed under the monoidal product
\otimes
in
\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{V}}
: For, given
S,S^{\prime}\in\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{V}}
, there is, in general, no map
DT(S\otimes S^{\prime})\to DT(S)\otimes DT(S^{\prime})
. And hence, if
(A,h),(A^{\prime},h^{\prime})
are algebras for
\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}
it is not possible to construct a map
DT(A\otimes A^{\prime})\to A\otimes A^{\prime}
.

To see this, consider an element of
T(A\otimes A^{\prime})_{\mathbf{2}}\subset T(A\otimes A^{\prime})_{\mathbf{2}}
represented by an
A\otimes A^{\prime}
structure on
\mathcal{L}^{2}
, where decoration by
A
is coloured in filled red, and decoration by
A^{\prime}
is unfilled blue:

Since the
TA
and
TA^{\prime}
components are both connected, this represents an element of
TA_{\mathbf{1}}\otimes TA^{\prime}_{\mathbf{1}}
(“delete” the black circle) and hence, applying
h,h^{\prime}
, we obtain an element of
A\otimes A^{\prime}
.

However, in the following
A\otimes A^{\prime}
structure on
\mathcal{L}^{3}
, the
TA
(red, filled) and
TA^{\prime}
(blue, unfilled) components are no longer connected:

Hence this does not represent an element of
(DTA\otimes DTA^{\prime})_{\mathbf{2}}
and so
\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{V}}^{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}\simeq{\bm{\mathfrak{MO}}}_{\mathsf{V}}
is not closed under the monoidal product
\otimes
on
\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{V}}
.

7.
A monad for circuit algebras

Despite the similarity between the definitions of the monads
\mathbb{T}
and
{\mathbb{T}^{\times}}
on
\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{V}}
, it is not possible to modify
\lambda_{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}
by replacing
\mathbb{T}
with
{\mathbb{T}^{\times}}
to obtain a distributive law for
\mathbb{D}
and
{\mathbb{T}^{\times}}
:

Namely, for all graphical species
S
, since
DS_{\mathbf{2}}\cong S_{\mathbf{2}}\amalg S_{\lx@sectionsign}
by definition, there is a canonical morphism

S(\shortmid\amalg\shortmid)=S_{\lx@sectionsign}\times S_{\lx@sectionsign}\to DS_{\mathbf{2}}\times DS_{\mathbf{2}}=DS(\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{2}}\amalg\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{2}})\to TDS_{\mathbf{2}\amalg\mathbf{2}}.

However, there is, in general, no morphism

(S_{\mathbf{2}}\amalg S_{\lx@sectionsign})\times(S_{\mathbf{2}}\amalg S_{\lx@sectionsign})\to TS_{\mathbf{2}\amalg\mathbf{2}}=DTS_{\mathbf{2}\amalg\mathbf{2}},

and therefore
\lambda_{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}\colon TD\Rightarrow DT
does not extend to a distributive law
{T^{\times}}D\Rightarrow D{T^{\times}}
.

The problem of finding the circuit algebra monad on
\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{V}}
is easily solved by observing that the monad
{\mathbb{T}^{\times}}
is, itself a composite: the free graded monoid monad on
\mathsf{psh}(\Sigma)
(see
Section

3.4
) induces a monad
\mathbb{L}=(L,\mu^{\mathbb{L}},\eta^{\mathbb{L}})
on
\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{V}}
whose algebras are graphical species in
\mathsf{V}
that are equipped with an external product. Moreover, there is a distributive law
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}}\colon TL\Rightarrow LT
such that
{\mathbb{T}^{\times}}=\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}
is the induced composite monad.

It will then be straightforward to prove (
Theorem

7.9
) that, for all (unpointed) graphical species
S
in
\mathsf{V}
,
LDTS
describes a circuit algebra in
\mathsf{V}
.

By
[
10
]
, there is a distributive law
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}(\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T})}\colon(DT)L\Rightarrow L(DT)
, and hence a composite monad
\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}
on
\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{V}}
, if there are pairwise distributive laws
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}}\colon TL\Rightarrow LT
, and
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}}\colon DL\Rightarrow LD
, such that the following
Yang-Baxter diagram
of endofunctors on
\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{V}}
commutes:

(7.1)

\textstyle{DTL\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}}}
\textstyle{DLT\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}}T}
\textstyle{TDL\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda_{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}L}
\scriptstyle{T\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}}}
\textstyle{LDT.}
\textstyle{TLD\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}}D}
\textstyle{LTD\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{L\lambda_{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}}

In
Section

7.1
, the monad
\mathbb{L}
is defined and the distributive laws
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}}:TL\Rightarrow LT
and
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}}:DL\Rightarrow LD
are constructed. In
Section

7.2
, it is proved that the diagram (
7.1
) commutes, and the EM category of algebras for the composite monad
\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}
on
\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{V}}
is canonically equivalent to the category
{\bm{\mathfrak{CA}}}_{\mathsf{V}}
of circuit algebras in
\mathsf{V}
.

7.1.
The free external product monad
\mathbb{L}

Recall from
Section

2.2
that, for any finite set
X
,
\mathrm{Core}(X/\mathsf{Set_{f}})
is the maximal subgroupoid of
X/\mathsf{Set_{f}}
whose objects are pairs
(Y,f)
with
f\in\mathsf{Set_{f}}(X,Y)
a morphism of finite sets.

As usual, let
\mathsf{V}
be a category with sufficient (co)limits. The free graded monoid endofunctor
L
on
\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{V}}
is given by

(7.2)

\begin{array}[]{lll}LS_{\lx@sectionsign}&=&S_{\lx@sectionsign}\\
LS_{X}&=&\mathrm{colim}_{(Y,f)\in\mathrm{Core}(X/\mathsf{Set_{f}})}\prod_{y\in Y}S_{f^{-1}(y)}\end{array}

for all finite sets
X
. This underlies a monad
\mathbb{L}=(L,\mu^{\mathbb{L}},\eta^{\mathbb{L}})
with monadic multiplication
\mu^{\mathbb{L}}
induced by disjoint union of partitions, and monadic unit
\eta^{\mathbb{L}}
induced by inclusion of trivial partitions.

The following lemma is immediate from the definitions (see also
Lemma

3.23
):

Lemma 7.3
.

The EM category of algebras for the monad
\mathbb{L}
on
\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{V}}
is equivalent to the category of graphical species with unital external product in
\mathsf{V}
. In particular
\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{V}}^{\mathbb{L}}
is equivalent to the monoid subcategory of
\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{V}}
.

In order to describe a distributive law
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}}\colon TL\Rightarrow
for
\mathbb{L}
and
\mathbb{T}
, observe first that
L\subset{T^{\times}}
is a subfunctor since, if
S
is a graphical species in
\mathsf{V}
, and
X
a finite set, then

\begin{array}[]{lll}LS_{X}&=&\mathrm{colim}_{(Y,f)\in\mathrm{Core}(X/\mathsf{Set_{f}})}\prod_{y\in Y}S_{f^{-1}(y)}\\
&=&\mathrm{colim}_{\mathcal{X}\in{{X}\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cor}_{\boxtimes}}_{\mathrm{iso}}}S(\mathcal{X})\end{array}

where
{{X}\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cor}_{\boxtimes}}_{\mathrm{iso}}\subset X{\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathrm{iso}}}
is the groupoid of
X
-graphs
\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{cor}}
that are disjoint unions of corollas
\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{cor}}=\coprod_{i\in I}{\mathcal{C}_{X_{i}}}
(with
\coprod_{i\in I}X_{i}=X
). In particular, from this point of view, the multiplication
\mu^{\mathbb{L}}
for
\mathbb{L}
is just the restriction of the multiplication
\mu^{{\mathbb{T}^{\times}}}
for
{\mathbb{T}^{\times}}
(
Proposition

5.14
) to
\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{cor}}
-shaped graphs of disjoint unions of corollas,
and the unit
\eta^{\mathbb{L}}
for
\mathbb{L}
, is the obvious corestriction.

Given a finite set
X
, and a connected
X
-graph
\mathcal{X}\in X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{CGr_{iso}}
, let
\mathsf{Cor}_{\boxtimes}^{(\mathcal{X})}
denote the full subgroupoid of
\mathsf{Gr}^{(\mathcal{X})}
whose objects are nondegenerate
\mathcal{X}
-shaped graphs of graphs
\mathbf{\Gamma}
such that, for all
(\mathcal{C}_{X_{b}},b)\in\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{X})}
,
\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{C}_{X_{b}},b)\in{{X}\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cor}_{\boxtimes}}_{\mathrm{iso}}
is a disjoint union of corollas. Then,

\begin{array}[]{lll}TLS_{X}&=&\mathrm{colim}_{\mathcal{X}\in X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{CGr_{iso}}}\mathrm{lim}_{(\mathcal{C},b)\in\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{X})}}LS(\mathcal{C})\\
&=&\mathrm{colim}_{\mathcal{X}\in X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{CGr_{iso}}}\mathrm{lim}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}\in\mathsf{Cor}_{\boxtimes}^{(\mathcal{X})}}S(\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{X})).\end{array}

Since, for all
\mathbf{\Gamma}\in\mathsf{Cor}_{\boxtimes}^{(\mathcal{X})}
, the colimit
\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{X})
has the structure of an admissible (in general, not connected)
X
-graph, there is a canonical morphism
TLS_{X}\to\mathrm{colim}_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\in X{\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathrm{iso}}}}S(\mathcal{X}^{\prime})
.
And, therefore, since every graph is the coproduct in
\mathsf{Gr}
of its connected components,
S(\mathcal{X}^{\prime})=\prod_{k\in K}S(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{k})
describes an element of
LTS_{X}
for all
X
-graphs
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}=\coprod_{k\in K}\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{k}
.

Let
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}}\colon TL\Rightarrow LT
be the natural transformation so obtained. In other words,
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}}
is induced by the canonical morphisms,

\mathrm{colim}_{\mathcal{X}\in X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{CGr_{iso}}}\left(\mathrm{colim}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}\in\mathsf{Cor}_{\boxtimes}^{(\mathcal{X})}}S(\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{X}))\right)\to\mathrm{colim}_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\in X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{CGr_{iso}}}S(\mathcal{X}^{\prime})\ (\text{where }S\in\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{V}},\text{ and }X\in{\mathbf{B}}),

that forgets pairs

(\mathcal{X},\mathbf{\Gamma})
where
\mathcal{X}
is a connected admissible
X
-graph and
\mathbf{\Gamma}\in\mathsf{Cor}_{\boxtimes}^{(\mathcal{X})}
, and remembers only the
X
-graphs given by the colimit
\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{X})=\coprod_{k\in K}\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{X})_{k}
, in the evaluation of
S(\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{X}))
.

Lemma 7.4
.

The natural transformation
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}}\colon TL\Rightarrow LT
satisfies the four axioms of a distributive law, and
\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}={\mathbb{T}^{\times}}
on
\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{V}}
. Hence, algebras for
\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}
are nonunital circuit algebras.

Proof.

Since a graph is the disjoint union of its connected components,
LT={T^{\times}}\colon\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{V}}\to\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{V}}
.

Moreover,
L\subset{T^{\times}}
and
T\subset{T^{\times}}
so
TL\subset({{T^{\times}}})^{2}
is a subfunctor, and
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}}
is just the restriction to
TL
of
\mu^{{\mathbb{T}^{\times}}}\colon({{T^{\times}}})^{2}\Rightarrow{T^{\times}}=LT
on
\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{V}}
. The four distributive law axioms then follow immediately from the monad axioms.
∎

Observe also that there is an obvious choice of natural transformation
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}}\colon DL\Rightarrow LD
that defines the distributive law for
\mathbb{L}
and
\mathbb{D}
:

Lemma 7.5
.

The natural transformation
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}}\colon DL\Rightarrow LD
obtained by extending
the natural transformation

L\eta^{\mathbb{D}}\colon L\Rightarrow LD
to a
DL
by

S_{\lx@sectionsign}\to DS_{\mathbf{2}}\xrightarrow{\eta^{\mathbb{L}}DS}LDS_{\mathbf{2}},\ \text{ and }S_{\lx@sectionsign}\to DS_{\mathbf{0}}\xrightarrow{\eta^{\mathbb{L}}DS}LDS_{\mathbf{0}},\ \text{ for all }S\in\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{V}}

describes a distributive law.

Proof.

Informally,
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}}
takes a tuple
(S_{X_{i}})_{i=1}^{n}\in LS_{\prod_{i}X_{i}}
of objects in the image of
S
, and regards it as a tuple in the image of
DS
, and takes the image of
S_{\lx@sectionsign}
in
DS_{\mathbf{0}}
or
DS_{\mathbf{2}}
and regards it as a 1-tuple in the image of
DS\hookrightarrow LDS
. This construction is natural in
S
and it is simple to show, using the monad unit axiom for
\eta^{\mathbb{D}}
, that
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}}
so obtained satisfies the axioms of a distributive law.
∎

7.2.
An iterated distributive law for circuit algebras

So far, we have defined three monads
\mathbb{T},\mathbb{D}
and
\mathbb{L}
on
\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{V}}
, and three distributive laws
\lambda_{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}\colon TD\Rightarrow DT
,
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}}\colon TL\Rightarrow LT
and
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}}\colon DL\Rightarrow LD
. It remains to show that the Yang-Baxter diagram (
7.1
) commutes and therefore there is an iterated distributive law for the three monads such that the resulting composite monad
\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}
on
\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{V}}
is the desired circuit algebra monad.

Let
\mathcal{G}
be any graph and
W\subset V_{2}
any subset of bivalent vertices of
\mathcal{G}
. As in
Section

6.3
, let
\mathcal{G}_{\setminus W}
be the graph obtained from
\mathcal{G}
by deleting the vertices in
W
. If
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}\colon\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{G}_{\setminus W})}\to\mathsf{Gr}
is a nondegenerate
\mathcal{G}_{\setminus W}
-shaped graph of graphs, then there is an induced nondegenerate
\mathcal{G}
-shaped graph of graphs
\mathbf{\Gamma}^{W}\colon\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{G})}\to\mathsf{Gr}
defined by

(7.6)

(\mathcal{C},b)\mapsto\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{2}}&(\mathcal{C},b)\text{ is a neighbourhood of }v\in W\\
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathcal{W}}(\mathsf{del}_{\setminus W}\circ b)&\text{ otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

Moreover,
\mathbf{\Gamma}^{W}
induces an inclusion
W\hookrightarrow V_{2}(\mathbf{\Gamma}^{W}(\mathcal{X}))
(see
Figure

13
), and

(7.7)

\mathbf{\Gamma}^{W}(\mathcal{X})_{\setminus W}=\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}(\mathcal{X}_{\setminus W}).

Hence, the assignment
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}\to\mathbf{\Gamma}^{W}
defines a canonical full embedding
\mathsf{Gr}^{(\mathcal{G}_{\setminus W})}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{Gr}^{(\mathcal{G})}
of categories that preserves similarity classes of colimits:

\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}
\mathbf{\Gamma}^{W}
\mathcal{G}_{\setminus W}
\mathcal{G}

Figure 13.
The colimits of the
\mathcal{G}
-shaped graph of graphs
\mathbf{\Gamma}^{W}
described in (
7.6
), and the
\mathcal{G}_{\setminus W}
-shaped graph of graphs
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}
are similar in
\mathsf{Gr}_{*}
.

In particular, the embedding
\mathsf{Gr}^{(\mathcal{G}_{\setminus W})}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{Gr}^{(\mathcal{G})}
restricts to an inclusion
\mathsf{Cor}_{\boxtimes}^{(\mathcal{G}_{\setminus W})}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{Cor}_{\boxtimes}^{(\mathcal{G})}
.

Proposition 7.8
.

The triple of distributive laws
\lambda_{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}\colon TD\Rightarrow DT
,
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}}\colon TL\Rightarrow LT
, and
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}}\colon DL\Rightarrow LD
on
\mathsf{GS}
, describe a composite monad
\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}
on
\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{V}}
.

Proof.

By
[
10
, Theorem 1.6]
, we must check that the diagram (
7.1
) commutes for
\lambda_{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}},\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}}
, and
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}}
:

For all graphical species
S
in
\mathsf{V}
and all finite sets
X
,

TDLS_{X}=\mathrm{colim}_{\mathcal{X}\in X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{CGr_{iso}}}\coprod_{W\subset(V_{0}\amalg V_{2})(\mathcal{X})}\mathrm{lim}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}\in\mathsf{Cor}_{\boxtimes}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(\mathcal{X}_{\setminus W})}}}S(\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}(\mathcal{X}_{\setminus W}))

is indexed by triples

(\mathcal{X},W,\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W})
, where
\mathcal{X}
is a connected
X
-graph,
W\subset V_{2}(\mathcal{X})
(or
\mathcal{X}\cong\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{0}}
and
W=V(\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{0}})
) and
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}
is a nondegenerate
\mathcal{X}_{\setminus W}
-shaped graph of graphs such that
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}(b)
is a disjoint union of corollas for each
(\mathcal{C}_{X_{b}},b)\in\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{X}_{\setminus W})}
.

Observe first that, if
W=V(\mathcal{X})
, then either
\mathcal{X}\cong\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{0}}
or
\mathcal{X}\cong\mathcal{W}^{m}
in
\mathbf{0}\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{CGr_{iso}}
, or
\mathcal{X}\cong\mathcal{L}^{k}
in
\mathbf{2}\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{CGr_{iso}}
. In each case
\mathcal{X}_{\setminus W}\cong(\shortmid)
and hence
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}
is the trivial graph of graphs
id\mapsto(\shortmid)
. In particular, the maps
S(\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}(\mathcal{X}_{\setminus W}))\to TDLS_{X}\to LDTS_{X}
induced by each path
TDL\Rightarrow LDT
in (
7.1
) coincide, since both factor through
S_{\lx@sectionsign}
.

Assume therefore that
W\neq V(\mathcal{X})
. The two paths in (
7.1
) are then described by the following data:

Top:

\textstyle{{\begin{array}[]{c}(\mathcal{X}_{\setminus W},\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathcal{W}}),\\
\mathcal{X}_{\setminus W}\in X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{CGr_{iso}}\end{array}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tiny{\text{ take colimit of }\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}}}
\textstyle{{\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}(\mathcal{X}_{\setminus W})\\
\in X{\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathrm{iso}}}\end{array}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{L\eta^{\mathbb{D}}T}
\textstyle{{\begin{array}[]{c}(\mathcal{X},W,\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}),\\
\mathcal{X}\in X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{CGr_{iso}}\end{array}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tiny{\text{delete }W\text{ in }\mathcal{X}}}
\textstyle{S(\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}(\mathcal{X}_{\setminus W}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{S(\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}(\mathcal{X}_{\setminus W}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}(\mathcal{X}_{\setminus W})\\
\in X{\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathrm{iso}}}\end{array}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{S(\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}(\mathcal{X}_{\setminus W}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{DTLS_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}}S}
\textstyle{DLTS_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}}TS}
\textstyle{S(\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}(\mathcal{X}_{\setminus W}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{TDLS_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda_{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}LS}
\textstyle{LDTS_{X}}

where, in each case, we evaluate
S(\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}(\mathcal{X}_{\setminus W}))
and take the appropriate colimit of such objects.

Bottom:

The bottom path of (
7.1
) gives

\textstyle{{\begin{array}[]{c}(\mathcal{X},W,\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}),\\
\mathcal{X}\in X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{CGr_{iso}}\end{array}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tiny{\text{ forget }W}}
\textstyle{{\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbf{\Gamma}^{W}(\mathcal{X})_{\setminus W}\\
\in X{\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathrm{iso}}}\end{array}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{S(\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}(\mathcal{X}_{\setminus W}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\begin{array}[]{c}(\mathcal{X},\mathbf{\Gamma}^{W},W),\\
\mathcal{X}\in X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{CGr_{iso}}\end{array}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tiny{\text{ take colimit of }\mathbf{\Gamma}^{W}}}
\textstyle{{\begin{array}[]{c}(\mathbf{\Gamma}^{W}(\mathcal{X}),W),\\
\mathbf{\Gamma}^{W}(\mathcal{X})\in X{\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathrm{iso}}}\end{array}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tiny{\text{ delete }W\text{ in }\mathbf{\Gamma}^{W}(\mathcal{X})}}
\textstyle{S(\mathbf{\Gamma}^{W}(\mathcal{X})_{\setminus W})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{TDLS_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T\lambda_{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{L}}S}
\textstyle{S(\mathbf{\Gamma}^{W}(\mathcal{X})_{\setminus W})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{S(\mathbf{\Gamma}^{W}(\mathcal{X})_{\setminus W})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{LDTS_{X}}
\textstyle{TLDS_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}}DS}
\textstyle{LTDS_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{L\lambda_{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}S}

where
\mathbf{\Gamma}^{W}
is the nondegenerate
\mathcal{X}
-shaped graph of graphs in
\mathsf{Cor}_{\boxtimes}^{(\mathcal{X})}
obtained from
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{/W}
as in (
7.6
).

So,
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\setminus W}(\mathcal{X}_{\setminus W})=\mathbf{\Gamma}^{W}(\mathcal{X})_{\setminus W}
by (
7.7
). Hence, the diagram (
7.1
) commutes, and
(\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}},\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}},\lambda_{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}})
describe an iterated distributive law for
\mathbb{L},\mathbb{D}
and
\mathbb{T}
on
\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{V}}
.
∎

It therefore remains to prove:

Theorem 7.9
.

The EM category of algebras for the composite monad
\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}
on
\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{V}}
is isomorphic to the category
{\bm{\mathfrak{CA}}}_{\mathsf{V}}
of circuit algebras in
\mathsf{V}
.

Proof.

By
[
5
]
, for a composite monad is equipped with an algebra structure for each of the component monads. Hence, an algebra
(A,h)
for the composite monad
\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}
on
\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{V}}
has an
\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}
-algebra structure
h^{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}}\colon LTA\to A
, and also a
\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}
algebra structure
h^{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}\colon DTA\to A
. Each structure is given by the corresponding restriction
LTA\to LDTA\xrightarrow{h}A
, respectively
DTA\to LDTA\xrightarrow{h}A
. Therefore, by
Lemma

7.4
,
h
induces an external product
\boxtimes
, and contraction
\zeta
on
A
such that
(A,\boxtimes,\zeta)
is a nonunital circuit algebra. And, by
Theorem

6.27
,
h
induces a unital multiplication
(\diamond,\epsilon)
on
A
, such that

\diamond_{X,Y}^{x\ddagger y}=\zeta_{X\amalg Y}^{x\ddagger y}\boxtimes_{X,Y}\colon(A_{X}\times A_{Y})^{x\ddagger y}\to A_{X\amalg Y\setminus\{x,y\}}.

Hence

\textstyle{A_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(id\times ch_{x})\circ\Delta}
\textstyle{(A_{X}\times A_{\lx@sectionsign})^{x\ddagger 2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{id\times\epsilon}
\textstyle{(A_{X}\times A_{\mathbf{2}})^{x\ddagger 2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\zeta^{x\ddagger 2}_{X\amalg\mathbf{2}}\circ\boxtimes}
\textstyle{A_{X}}

is the identity on
A_{X}
. So,
(A,h)
has a canonical circuit algebra structure.

Conversely, a circuit algebra
(S,\boxtimes,\zeta,\epsilon)
in
\mathsf{V}
has an underlying modular operad structure
(S,\diamond,\zeta,\epsilon)
, where
\diamond_{X,Y}^{x\ddagger y}=\zeta_{X\amalg Y}^{x\ddagger y}\boxtimes_{X,Y}
and hence
(S,\boxtimes,\zeta,\epsilon)
defines a
\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}
-algebra structure
h^{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}\colon DTS\to S
by
Theorem

6.27
. By
Lemma

7.3
,
(S,\boxtimes)
has an
\mathbb{L}
-algebra structure where
h^{\mathbb{L}}\colon LS\to S
is given by
\boxtimes_{X,Y}\colon S_{X}\times S_{Y}
on
S_{X}\times S_{Y}
. It follows immediately from the circuit algebra axioms that
h^{\mathbb{L}}\circ Lh^{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}\colon LDTS\to S
gives
S
the structure of an
\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}
-algebra.

These maps are functorial by construction and extend to an equivalence
{\bm{\mathfrak{CA}}}_{\mathsf{V}}\simeq\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{V}}^{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}
.∎

Corollary 7.10
.

If
\mathsf{V}=\mathsf{Set}
, then for each palette
(\mathfrak{C},\omega)
in
\mathsf{Set}
, the subcategory
\mathfrak{CA}^{(\mathfrak{C},\omega)}
of
(\mathfrak{C},\omega)
-coloured circuit algebras is the EM-category of algebras for the monad
\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}
restricted to
{\mathsf{GS}^{(\mathfrak{C},\omega)}}
. By
Proposition

3.27
, this is equivalent to the category
\mathsf{Alg}_{\mathsf{Set}}(\mathrm{WD}^{(\mathfrak{C},\omega)})={\mathsf{CA}}^{(\mathfrak{C},\omega)}
of
\mathsf{Set}
-valued algebras for the operad
\mathrm{WD}^{(\mathfrak{C},\omega)}
of
(\mathfrak{C},\omega)
-coloured wiring diagrams defined in
[
42
, Section 5.2]
.

Example 7.11
.

By
Definition

3.33
and
Theorem

7.9
, the category
{\bm{\mathfrak{WP}}}_{\mathsf{V}}
of wheeled props in
\mathsf{V}
is isomorphic to the slice category
\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{V}}^{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}/Di^{\mathsf{V}}
. In particular – as in Examples
5.19
and
6.28
– the constructions of Sections
7.1
and
7.2
may be modified, in terms of directed graphs, to obtain oriented versions of the monads and distributive laws and a composite wheeled prop monad on
{\mathsf{OGS}_{\mathsf{V}}}
.

Remark 7.12
.

Like monads, operads encode algebraic structure. Indeed, for any operad in
\mathsf{Set}
, one may define a monad with the same algebras. Markl
[
37
]
defined distributive laws for operads and proved that the operad obtained by composing Koszul operads via an
operad distributive law
is Koszul. This result was used in
[
29
]
to compose an operad (of downward wiring diagrams
[
42
, Section 5.1]
) associated to the restriction of
\mathbb{T}
to
{\mathsf{GS}^{\{*\}}}
and an operad associated to the restriction of
\mathbb{L}
to
{\mathsf{GS}^{\{*\}}}
. So, to determine whether the operad (of wiring diagrams) for monochrome unital circuit algebras obtained from
\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}
is Koszul, it should be sufficient to explore whether the
operad obtained by restricting
\mathbb{D}
is. This is not expected to be the case, and the question is not further explored here.

8.
A nerve theorem for circuit algebras

This section recalls the nerve theorem for
\mathsf{Set}
-valued modular operads
[
41
, Theorem 8.2]
and gives a proof, by the same methods, of an analogous result for
\mathsf{Set}
-valued circuit algebras.

8.1.
Overview of the method

Let
\mathsf{C}\mathsf{Gr}_{*}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}
be the full subcategory of connected graphs and pointed étale morphisms from
Section

6.3
, that is obtained in the bo-ff factorisation of the functor
\mathsf{C}\mathsf{Gr}\to\mathsf{GS}\to\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}
, and, as in
Section

6.4
, let
\mathbb{T}_{*}
be the monad on
\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}
whose EM category of algebras is equivalent to the category
\bm{\mathfrak{MO}}
of modular operads (in
\mathsf{Set}
).

So, there is a commuting diagram of functors

(8.1)

\textstyle{\Xi\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
f.f.
\textstyle{\bm{\mathfrak{MO}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\text{forget}^{\mathbb{T}_{*}}}
\scriptstyle{N}
\textstyle{\mathsf{psh}(\Xi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j^{*}}
\textstyle{{{\mathbf{B}}}^{\scriptstyle{\bm{\lx@sectionsign}}}_{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
f.f.
\textstyle{\mathsf{C}\mathsf{Gr}_{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
f.f.
\scriptstyle{j}
b.o.
\textstyle{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\text{forget}^{\mathbb{D}}}

f.f.
\scriptstyle{\text{free}^{\mathbb{T}_{*}}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{psh}(\mathsf{C}\mathsf{Gr}_{*})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{{\mathbf{B}}}^{\scriptstyle{\bm{\lx@sectionsign}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
f.f.
dense
b.o.
\textstyle{\mathsf{C}\mathsf{Gr}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
f.f.
b.o.
\textstyle{\mathsf{GS}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

f.f.
\scriptstyle{\text{free}^{\mathbb{D}}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{psh}(\mathsf{C}\mathsf{Gr})}

where
\Xi
is the category, whose objects are indexed by connected graphs, obtained in bo-ff factorisation of the functor
\mathsf{C}\mathsf{Gr}\to\mathsf{GS}\to\bm{\mathfrak{MO}}
.

In
[
41
, Section 8]
, it was shown (for
\mathsf{V}=\mathsf{Set}
) that, though the modular operad monad
\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}
on
\mathsf{GS}
does not have arities, the monad
\mathbb{T}_{*}
on
\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}
does, whereby the following theorem holds:

Theorem 8.2
.

[
41
, Theorem 8.2]

The monad
\mathbb{T}_{*}
, and the category
\mathsf{C}\mathsf{Gr}_{*}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}
, satisfy the conditions of
[
6
]
(
\mathbb{T}_{*}
has arities
\mathsf{C}\mathsf{Gr}_{*}
). Hence, the nerve
N\colon\bm{\mathfrak{MO}}\to\mathsf{psh}(\Xi)
is fully faithful.
Its essential image consists of those presheaves
P
on
\Xi
such that

P(\mathcal{G})=\mathrm{lim}_{(\mathcal{C},b)\in\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{G})}}P(\mathcal{C})
for all graphs
\mathcal{G}
.

The circuit algebra monad
\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}
on
\mathsf{GS}
also does not have arities. However, since
\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}_{*}}\cong\mathsf{GS}^{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}
(
Proposition

6.25
) and
\mathbb{L}
lifts functorially to
\mathsf{GS}^{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}
, the distributive law
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}}\colon TL\Rightarrow LT
on
\mathsf{GS}
extends to a distributive law
T_{*}\widetilde{L}\Rightarrow\widetilde{L}T_{*}
for
\mathbb{T}_{*}
and the lift
\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}
of
\mathbb{L}
to
\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}
. By
Theorem

7.9
, the EM category of algebras for this composite monad
{\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}
on
\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}
is precisely the category
{\bm{\mathfrak{CA}}}
of circuit algebras in
\mathsf{Set}
.

By
Section

6.3
(see also
[
41
, Section 7.3]
), the full subcategory
\mathsf{Gr}_{*}\subset\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}
of all graphs and pointed morphisms induces a fully faithful inclusion
\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{psh}(\mathsf{Gr}_{*})
. Hence, if
{\Xi^{\times}}
is the category obtained in the bo-ff factorisation of
\mathsf{Gr}\to\mathsf{GS}\to{\bm{\mathfrak{CA}}}
. we may consider the following diagram of functors in which the left and middle squares commute, and the right squares commute up to natural isomorphism:

(8.3)

\textstyle{{{\Xi^{\times}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
f.f.
\textstyle{{\bm{\mathfrak{CA}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\text{forget}^{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}}
\scriptstyle{N_{{{\Xi^{\times}}}}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{psh}({{\Xi^{\times}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j^{*}}
\textstyle{{{\mathbf{B}}}^{\scriptstyle{\bm{\lx@sectionsign}}}_{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
f.f.
\textstyle{\mathsf{Gr}_{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
f.f.
\scriptstyle{j}
b.o.
\textstyle{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\text{forget}^{\mathbb{D}}}

f.f.
\scriptstyle{\text{free}^{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{psh}(\mathsf{Gr}_{*})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{{\mathbf{B}}}^{\scriptstyle{\bm{\lx@sectionsign}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
f.f.
dense
b.o.
\textstyle{\mathsf{Gr}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
f.f.
b.o.
\textstyle{\mathsf{GS}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

f.f.
\scriptstyle{\text{free}^{\mathbb{D}}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{psh}(\mathsf{Gr}).}

The remainder of this paper is dedicated to proving the following theorem using a modification of the proof of
Theorem

8.2
(
[
41
, Theorem 8.2]
) to show that
\mathsf{Gr}_{*}
provides arities for
{\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}
:

Theorem 8.4
.

The functor
N\colon{\bm{\mathfrak{CA}}}\to\mathsf{psh}({\Xi^{\times}})
is full and faithful. Its essential image consists of precisely those presheaves
P
on
{\Xi^{\times}}
such that

(8.5)

P(\mathcal{G})=\mathrm{lim}_{(\mathcal{C},b)\in\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{G})}}P(\mathcal{C})\text{ for all graphs }\mathcal{G}.

Recall that the category
{\bm{\mathfrak{WP}}}=\mathsf{GS}^{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}/Di\cong\mathsf{OGS}^{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}
is the category of wheeled props in
\mathsf{Set}
. Hence,
Theorem

8.4
implies an abstract nerve theorem for wheeled props.
For, let
\mathsf{O}{\Xi^{\times}}\overset{\textup{def}}{=}{\Xi^{\times}}/Di
be the induced oriented graphical category. Since
\mathsf{psh}(\mathbf{G})/Di\simeq\mathsf{psh}(\mathbf{G}/Di)
for
\mathbf{G}=\mathsf{Gr},\mathsf{Gr}_{*},{\Xi^{\times}}
, it follows that:

Corollary 8.6
.

The induced functor
\mathsf{O}N\colon{\bm{\mathfrak{WP}}}\to\mathsf{psh}(\mathsf{O}{\Xi^{\times}})
is full and faithful. Its essential image consists of precisely those presheaves
P
on
\mathsf{O}{\Xi^{\times}}
that restrict to graphical species.

Sections
8.2
-
8.4
are devoted to constructing the graphical category
{\Xi^{\times}}
and giving a direct proof of
Theorem

8.4
, in the style of
Theorem

8.2
(see also,
[
41
, Theorem 8.2]
). Further proofs, using results from
[
6
]
and
[
11
]
on strongly cartesian monads, are outlined in Remarks
8.17
and
8.18
.

8.2.
The monad
\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}
on
{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{\mathsf{V}}

The first step is to understand the monad
\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}
on
{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{\mathsf{V}}
. Here
\mathsf{V}
is, as usual, an arbitrary category with sufficient limits and colimits.

The lift
\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}
of
\mathbb{L}
to
{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{\mathsf{V}}
is given by
\widetilde{L}(S_{*})=(LS,\eta^{\mathbb{L}}\epsilon,\eta^{\mathbb{L}}o)
, for all pointed graphical species
S_{*}=(S,\epsilon,o)
in
\mathsf{V}
(where
\mu^{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}},\eta^{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}}
are induced by
\mu^{\mathbb{L}}
and
\eta^{\mathbb{L}}
in the obvious way.)

Let
X
be a finite set. The category
{X}\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{CGr}_{\mathsf{sim}}
of similar connected
X
-graphs was defined in
Section

6.4
and the monad
\mathbb{T}_{*}=(T_{*},\mu^{\mathbb{T}_{*}},\eta^{\mathbb{T}_{*}})
with
T_{*}S_{X}=\mathrm{colim}_{\mathcal{X}\in{X}\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{CGr}_{\mathsf{sim}}}S(\mathcal{X})
was discussed in
Section

6.4
.

Similarity morphisms preserve connected components. Hence the category
X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathsf{sim}}
of
all

X
-graphs and similarity morphisms, may be defined by

(8.7)

X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathsf{sim}}\overset{\textup{def}}{=}\mathrm{colim}_{(Y,f)\in\mathrm{Core}(X/\mathsf{Set_{f}})}\coprod_{y\in Y}\left(f^{-1}(y)\right){X}\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{CGr}_{\mathsf{sim}}.

The endofunctor
{\widetilde{L}T_{*}}
is described by the obvious quotient of
{T^{\times}}
: Let
S_{*}=(S,\epsilon,o)
be a pointed graphical species in
\mathsf{V}
and let
X
be a finite set, then

(8.8)

\begin{array}[]{lll}{{\widetilde{L}T_{*}}}S_{X}&=&\mathrm{colim}_{(Y,f)\in\mathrm{Core}(X/\mathsf{Set_{f}})}\left(\prod_{y\in Y}T_{*}S_{f^{-1}(y)}\right)\\
&=&\mathrm{colim}_{(Y,f)\in\mathrm{Core}(X/\mathsf{Set_{f}})}\left(\prod_{y\in Y}\left(\mathrm{colim}_{(\mathcal{G},\rho)\in{(f^{-1}(y))}\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{CGr}_{\mathsf{sim}}}S(\mathcal{G})\right)\right)\\
&=&\mathrm{colim}_{\mathcal{X}\in X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathsf{sim}}}S(\mathcal{X}).\end{array}

And,

\begin{array}[]{lll}T_{*}\widetilde{L}S_{X}&=&\mathrm{colim}_{\mathcal{X}\in{X}\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{CGr}_{\mathsf{sim}}}\mathrm{lim}_{(\mathcal{C},b)\in\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{X})}}LS(\mathcal{C})\\
&=&\mathrm{colim}_{\mathcal{X}\in{X}\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{CGr}_{\mathsf{sim}}}\mathrm{lim}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}\in\mathsf{Cor}_{\boxtimes}^{(\mathcal{X})}}S(\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{X})).\end{array}

As
{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{\mathsf{V}}^{\mathbb{T}_{*}}\cong\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{V}}^{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}
and
\mathbb{L}
lifts to
\mathsf{GS}_{\mathsf{V}}^{\mathbb{D}\mathbb{T}}
, there is a distributive law
T_{*}\widetilde{L}\Rightarrow{\widetilde{L}T_{*}}
, induced by the distributive law
\lambda_{\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}}\colon TL\Rightarrow LT={T^{\times}}
, which is compatible with similarity morphisms by construction: for each finite set
X
, a colimit indexed by pairs

(\mathcal{X},\mathbf{\Gamma})
of a connected admissible
X
-graph
\mathcal{X}
, and
\mathbf{\Gamma}\in\mathsf{Cor}_{\boxtimes}^{(\mathcal{X})}
, is replaced by a
colimit indexed over
(in general not connected) admissible
X
-graphs
\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{X})
.

8.3.
A graphical category for circuit algebras

For the remainder of the paper,
\mathsf{V}=\mathsf{Set}
.

The category
\mathsf{Gr}_{*}
obtained in the bo-ff factorisation of
\mathsf{Gr}\to\mathsf{GS}\to\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}
has been discussed in
Section

6.3
. Let
{\Xi^{\times}}
be the category, whose objects are graphs, obtained in the bo-ff factorisation of
\mathsf{Gr}\to\mathsf{GS}\to{\bm{\mathfrak{CA}}}
(and also of
\mathsf{Gr}_{*}\to\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}\to{\bm{\mathfrak{CA}}}
). This is the full subcategory of the Kleisli category
{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{{\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}}
of
{\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}
on free circuit algebras of the form
{\widetilde{L}T_{*}}{\Upsilon_{*}}\mathcal{G}=LDT\Upsilon\mathcal{G}
for graphs
\mathcal{G}\in\mathsf{Gr}
.
By definition, morphisms
\beta\in{\Xi^{\times}}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H})
are given by morphisms in
\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}({\Upsilon_{*}}\mathcal{G},{\widetilde{L}T_{*}}{\Upsilon_{*}}\mathcal{H})
.

To understand
{\Xi^{\times}}
, the first step is therefore to describe
{\widetilde{L}T_{*}}{\Upsilon_{*}}\mathcal{H}
for all graphs
\mathcal{H}
.

Since each connected component of
X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathsf{sim}}
contains an (in general, not connected) admissible
X
-graph
\mathcal{X}
, it follows from (
8.8
) that, for all finite sets
X
, elements of
({\widetilde{L}T_{*}}{\Upsilon_{*}}\mathcal{H})_{X}\cong{\Xi^{\times}}({\mathcal{C}_{X}},\mathcal{H})
are represented by pairs
(\mathcal{X},f)
where
\mathcal{X}
is an admissible
X
-graph and
f\in\mathsf{Gr}_{*}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{H})
. In particular,
f
factors uniquely as a morphism
\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{X}_{\setminus W_{f}}
in
\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathsf{sim}}
followed by a morphism
f^{\bot}\in\mathsf{Gr}(\mathcal{X}_{\setminus W_{f}},\mathcal{H})
.
Pairs
(\mathcal{X}^{1},f^{1})
and
(\mathcal{X}^{1},f^{2})
represent the same element
[\mathcal{X},f]_{*}\in({\widetilde{L}T_{*}}{\Upsilon_{*}}\mathcal{H})_{X}
if and only if there is an object
\mathcal{X}_{f}\in X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathsf{sim}}
and a commuting diagram

(8.9)

\textstyle{\mathcal{X}^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g^{1}}
\scriptstyle{f^{1}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}^{f}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\bot}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathcal{X}^{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g^{2}}
\scriptstyle{f^{2}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}}

in
\mathsf{Gr}_{*}
such that
g^{j}
are similarity morphisms in
X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathsf{sim}}
for
j=1,2
, and
f^{\bot}\in\mathsf{Gr}(\mathcal{X}^{f},\mathcal{H})
is an (unpointed) étale morphism.

In particular, for all
\mathcal{H}
,
e\in E(\mathcal{H})
and
m\geq 1
, the following special case of (
8.9
) commutes in
\mathsf{Gr}_{*}
:

(8.10)

\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{z}
\scriptstyle{ch_{e}\circ z}
\textstyle{(\shortmid)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{ch_{e}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{W}^{m}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa}
\scriptstyle{ch_{e}\circ\kappa}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}.}

Since
{\Xi^{\times}}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H})\cong{\widetilde{L}T_{*}}{\Upsilon_{*}}\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{G})=\mathrm{lim}_{(\mathcal{C},b)\in\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{G})}}{\widetilde{L}T_{*}}{\Upsilon_{*}}\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{C})
, morphisms
\gamma\in{\Xi^{\times}}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H})
are represented by pairs
(\mathbf{\Gamma},f)
, where
\mathbf{\Gamma}:\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{G})}\to\mathsf{Gr}
is a nondegenerate
\mathcal{G}
-shaped graph of graphs, and
f\in\mathsf{Gr}_{*}(\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{G}),\mathcal{H})
is a morphism in
\mathsf{Gr}_{*}
(see
[
41
, Section 8.1]
).

The following lemma generalises
[
41
, Lemma 8.9]
, and is proved in exactly the same manner.

Lemma 8.11
.

Let
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{H}
be graphs and, for
i=1,2
, let
\mathbf{\Gamma}^{i}
be a nondegenerate
\mathcal{G}
-shaped graph of graphs with colimit
\mathbf{\Gamma}^{i}(\mathcal{G})
, and
f^{i}\in\mathsf{Gr}_{*}(\mathbf{\Gamma}^{i}(\mathcal{G}),\mathcal{H})
.
Then
(\mathbf{\Gamma}^{1},f^{1}),(\mathbf{\Gamma}^{2},f^{2})
represent the same element
\gamma
of
{\Xi^{\times}}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H})
if and only if there is a representative
(\mathbf{\Gamma},f)
of
\gamma
, and a commuting diagram in
\mathsf{Gr}_{*}
of the following form:

(8.12)

\textstyle{\mathbf{\Gamma}^{1}(\mathcal{G})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{1}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{G})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathbf{\Gamma}^{2}(\mathcal{G})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{2}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H},}

where the morphisms in the top row are vertex deletion morphisms (including
z\colon\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{0}}\to(\shortmid)
) in
\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathsf{sim}}
, and
f\in\mathsf{Gr}(\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{G}),\mathcal{H})
.

The following terminology is from
[
27
]
.

Definition 8.13
.

A
(pointed) free morphism in
{\Xi^{\times}}
is a morphism in the image of the inclusion
\mathsf{Gr}_{*}\hookrightarrow{\Xi^{\times}}
. An
unpointed free morphism
in
{\Xi^{\times}}
is a morphism in the image of
\mathsf{Gr}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{Gr}_{*}\hookrightarrow{\Xi^{\times}}
. A
refinement morphism
[\mathbf{\Gamma}]
in
{\Xi^{\times}}
is a morphism in
{\Xi^{\times}}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H})
with a representative of the form
(\mathbf{\Gamma},id_{\mathcal{H}})
.

So, a free morphism in
{\Xi^{\times}}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H})
has a representative of the form
(id,f)
where
id\colon\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{G})}\to\mathsf{Gr}
is the trivial identity
\mathcal{G}
-shaped graph of graphs with colimit
\mathcal{G}
and
f\in\mathsf{Gr}_{*}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H})
. Refinements
[\mathbf{\Gamma}]
in
{\Xi^{\times}}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H})
are described by nondegenerate
\mathcal{G}
-shaped graph of graphs
\mathbf{\Gamma}
with colimit
\mathcal{H}
, and hence induce isomorphisms
E_{0}(\mathcal{G})\xrightarrow{\cong}E_{0}(\mathcal{H})
on boundaries.

In particular, morphisms in
{\Xi^{\times}}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H})
factor as
\mathcal{G}\xrightarrow{[\mathbf{\Gamma}]}\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{G})\xrightarrow{\mathsf{del}_{\setminus W}}\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{G})_{\setminus W}\xrightarrow{f}\mathcal{H}
. Here
\mathbf{\Gamma}\colon\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{G})}\to\mathsf{Gr}
is a nondegenerate
\mathcal{G}
-shaped graph of graphs with colimit
\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{G})\in\mathsf{Gr}
, and
f\in\mathsf{Gr}(\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{G})_{\setminus W},\mathcal{H})
is an (unpointed) étale morphism
\mathsf{Gr}
.

8.4.
Factorisation categories and the nerve theorem

Let
{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{{\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}}
be the Kleisli category of
{\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}
on
\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}
. By
[
6
, Proposition 2.5]
, to show that the monad
{\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}
, together with the category
\mathsf{Gr}_{*}
satisfy the conditions of the nerve theorem
[
6
, Theorem 1.10]
(that is,
{\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}
has arities
\mathsf{Gr}_{*}
) and therefore the nerve functor
N\colon{\bm{\mathfrak{CA}}}\to\mathsf{psh}({\Xi^{\times}})
is fully faithful, it is sufficient to show that certain categories, obtained by factoring morphisms in
{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{{\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}}
, are connected.

To construct these categories, let
S_{*}
be a pointed graphical species in
\mathsf{Set}
,
\mathcal{G}
a graph in
\mathsf{Gr}
, and let
\beta\in{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{{\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}}(\mathcal{G},S_{*})
be a morphism in the Kleisli category of
{\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}
. By the Yoneda lemma,

{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{{\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}}(\mathcal{G},S_{*})\cong\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}(\mathcal{G},{\widetilde{L}T_{*}}S_{*})\cong{\widetilde{L}T_{*}}S_{*}(\mathcal{G})=\mathrm{\mathrm{colim}_{(\mathcal{C},b)\in\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{G})}}}{\widetilde{L}T_{*}}S_{*}(\mathcal{C})

where

{\widetilde{L}T_{*}}S_{*}(\mathcal{C})\cong\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}S_{\lx@sectionsign}&\mathcal{C}\cong(\shortmid),\\
\mathrm{colim}_{\mathcal{X}\in X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathsf{sim}}}S(\mathcal{X})&\mathcal{C}\cong{\mathcal{C}_{X}}.\end{array}\right.

So,
\beta\in{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{\mathbb{T}_{*}}(\mathcal{G},S_{*})
is represented by pairs
(\mathbf{\Gamma},\alpha)
of

•

a nondegenerate
\mathcal{G}
-shaped graph of graphs
\mathbf{\Gamma}
with colimit
\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{G})
, viewed as a refinement
[\mathbf{\Gamma}]\in{\Xi^{\times}}(\mathcal{G},\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{G}))={\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{\mathbb{T}_{*}}(\mathcal{G},\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{G}))
,

•

an element
\alpha\in S(\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{G}))
viewed, by the Yoneda lemma, as a morphism
\alpha\in{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{\mathbb{T}_{*}}({\Upsilon_{*}}\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{G}),S_{*})

such that the induced composition
\mathcal{G}\xrightarrow{[\mathbf{\Gamma}]}\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{G})\xrightarrow{\alpha}S(\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathcal{G}))\twoheadrightarrow({\widetilde{L}T_{*}}S_{*})(\mathcal{G})
is precisely
\beta
.

Definition 8.14
.

Objects of the
factorisation category

\mathsf{fact}_{*}(\beta)
of
\beta
are pairs
(\mathbf{\Gamma},\alpha)
that represent
\beta
as above, and morphisms in
\mathsf{fact}_{*}(\beta)((\mathbf{\Gamma}^{1},\alpha^{1}),(\mathbf{\Gamma}^{2},\alpha^{2}))
are commuting diagrams in
{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{{\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}}

(8.15)

\textstyle{{[\mathbf{\Gamma}^{1}]}(\mathcal{G})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\scriptstyle{\alpha^{1}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{S_{*}}
\textstyle{{[\mathbf{\Gamma}^{2}]}(\mathcal{G})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha^{2}}

such that
g
is a morphism in
\mathsf{Gr}_{*}\hookrightarrow{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{{\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}}
.

Lemma 8.16
.

For all pointed graphical species
S_{*}
in
\mathsf{Set}
, all graphs
\mathcal{G}\in\mathsf{Gr}
, and all
\beta\in\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}(\mathcal{G},{\widetilde{L}T_{*}}S)
, the category
\mathsf{fact}_{*}(\beta)
is connected.

Proof.

If
\mathcal{G}\cong(\shortmid)
, then
\mathbf{\Gamma}
is isomorphic to the identity
(\shortmid)
-shaped graph of graphs.
If
\mathcal{G}\cong{\mathcal{C}_{X}}
for some finite set
X
, and
\beta\in{\widetilde{L}T_{*}}S_{X}\cong{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{\mathbb{T}_{*}}(\mathcal{G},S_{*})
, then objects of
\mathsf{fact}_{*}(\beta)
have the form
(\mathcal{X},\alpha)
where
\mathcal{X}\in X{\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathrm{iso}}}
is an admissible (in general not connected)
X
-graph,
\alpha\in S_{*}(\mathcal{X})
and
\beta
is the image of
\alpha
under the universal morphism
S_{*}(\mathcal{X})\to({\widetilde{L}T_{*}}S_{*})_{X}
. Since
({\widetilde{L}T_{*}}S_{*})_{X}=\mathrm{colim}_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\in\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathsf{sim}}}S(\mathcal{X}^{\prime})
, if
(\mathcal{X}^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})
also represents
\beta
, then
\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
are connected in
\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathsf{sim}}
and hence they are similar in
\mathsf{Gr}_{*}
.
Hence, if
\mathcal{G}=\mathcal{C}
is in the image of
{{\mathbf{B}}}^{\scriptstyle{\bm{\lx@sectionsign}}}_{*}\subset\mathsf{Gr}_{*}
, then
\mathsf{fact}_{*}(\beta)
is connected for all
\beta\in{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{\mathbb{T}_{*}}(\mathcal{C},S_{*})\cong{\widetilde{L}T_{*}}S(\mathcal{C})
.

For general
\mathcal{G}\in\mathsf{Gr}_{*}
, and
\beta\in{\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}}_{\mathbb{T}_{*}}(\mathcal{G},S_{*})
, observe first that, since colimits of graphs of graphs are computed componentwise, we may assume that
\mathcal{G}
is connected. Assume also that
\mathcal{G}\not\cong\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{0}}
. Let
(\mathbf{\Gamma}^{i},\alpha^{i})
,
i=1,2
be objects of
\mathsf{fact}_{*}(\beta)
. Then, for each
({\mathcal{C}_{X}},b)\in\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{G})}
,
\mathbf{\Gamma}^{1}(b)
and
\mathbf{\Gamma}^{2}(b)
are in the same connected component of
X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathsf{sim}}
, since the lemma holds when
\mathcal{G}\cong{\mathcal{C}_{X}}
. Therefore,
\mathbf{\Gamma}^{1}(\mathcal{G})
and
\mathbf{\Gamma}^{2}(\mathcal{G})
are similar in
\mathsf{Gr}_{*}
.
∎

Theorem

8.4
now follows from
[
6
, Sections 1 & 2]
.

Proof of
Theorem

8.4
.

The induced nerve functor
N\colon{\bm{\mathfrak{CA}}}\to\mathsf{psh}({\Xi^{\times}})
is fully faithful by
Lemma

8.16
,
[
6
, Proposition 2.5]
, and
[
6
, Propositions 1.5 & 1.9]
.

Moreover, by
[
6
, Theorem 1.10]
, its essential image is the subcategory of those presheaves on
{\Xi^{\times}}
whose restriction to
\mathsf{Gr}_{*}
are in the image of the fully faithful embedding
\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{psh}(\mathsf{Gr}_{*})
. So, a presheaf
P
on
{\Xi^{\times}}
described the nerve of a circuit algebra if and only if

P(\mathcal{G})\cong\mathrm{lim}_{(\mathcal{C},b)\in\mathsf{el}_{*}{(\mathcal{G})}}P(\mathcal{C})
, and hence, by finality of
\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{G})}\subset\mathsf{el}_{*}{(\mathcal{G})}
,

P(\mathcal{G})=\mathrm{lim}_{(\mathcal{C},b)\in\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{G})}}P(\mathcal{C}).
Therefore, the Segal condition (
8.5
) is satisfied and
Theorem

8.4
is proved.
∎

Following from Remarks
2.20
and
2.19
,
Theorem

8.4
also admits proofs using extensions of strongly cartesian monads as described in
[
6
, Proposition 4.4]
, and extendable algebraic patterns
[
11
]
:

Remark 8.17
.

Recall
Remark

2.19
.
By (
6.1
), for all finite sets
X
,

{T^{\times}}S_{X}=\mathrm{colim}_{\mathcal{X}\in X{\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{CGr_{iso}}}}S(\mathcal{X})=\mathrm{colim}_{\mathcal{X}\in X{\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{CGr_{iso}}}}\mathrm{lim}_{(\mathcal{C},b)\in\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{X})}}S(\mathcal{C}).

So (e.g., by
[
11
, Section 1]
)
{\mathbb{T}^{\times}}
is strongly cartesian with canonical arities
\mathsf{Gr}\subset\mathsf{GS}
.

By
Section

6.2
, the right adjoint functor
i^{*}\colon\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}\to\mathsf{GS}
associated to the monad
\mathbb{D}
on
\mathsf{GS}
is just the pullback to presheaves of the bijective on objects inclusion
i\colon{{\mathbf{B}}}^{\scriptstyle{\bm{\lx@sectionsign}}}\hookrightarrow{{\mathbf{B}}}^{\scriptstyle{\bm{\lx@sectionsign}}}_{*}
. And, by construction,
(LT)i^{*}=i^{*}({\widetilde{L}T_{*}})\colon\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}\to\mathsf{GS}
so the monad
{\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}
on
\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}
is the pushforward along
i
of
\mathbb{L}\mathbb{T}={\mathbb{T}^{\times}}
on
\mathsf{GS}
. Hence, by
[
6
, Proposition 4.4]
,
{\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}
on
\mathsf{\mathsf{GS}}_{*}
is strongly cartesian with canonical arities
\mathsf{Gr}_{*}
.

Remark 8.18
.

Recall Remarks
2.11
and
2.20
.
By (
8.8
), for all finite sets
X
,

\begin{array}[]{lll}{{\widetilde{L}T_{*}}}S_{X}&=&\mathrm{colim}_{\mathcal{X}\in X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathsf{sim}}}S(\mathcal{X})\\
&=&\mathrm{colim}_{\mathcal{X}\in X\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Gr}_{\mathsf{sim}}}\mathrm{lim}_{(\mathcal{C},b)\in\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{X})}}S(\mathcal{C})\end{array}

And, by the previous discussion, a morphism in
{\Xi^{\times}}
factor as a refinement (induced by a graphs of graphs) followed by a
\mathsf{Gr}_{*}
morphism.

Hence (e.g., by
[
11
, Section 1]
)
{\Xi^{\times}}
describes an extendable algebraic pattern, and

{\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{T}_{*}}
is the associated strongly cartesian monad, with canonical arities
\mathsf{Gr}_{*}
.

Theorem

8.4
then follows immediately from
[
6
,
52
]
.

It remains, finally to remark on the homotopy theory of circuit algebras (see also the Introduction).

Remark 8.19
.

Both the nerve theorem
[
41
, Theorem 8.2]
for modular operads on which
Theorem

8.4
is built, as well as Hackney, Robertson and Yau’s nerve theorem
[
24
, 3.6]
, lead to well-defined models of
(\infty,1)
-modular operads in which the fibrant objects in the appropriate category of presheaves are precisely those that satisfy the weak Segal condition: in the case of
[
41
]
it followed from the results of
[
9
]
that these were given by
P\colon\Xi^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathsf{sSet}
such that, for all (connected) graphs
\mathcal{G}\in\Xi
,

P(\mathcal{G})\simeq\mathrm{lim}_{(\mathcal{C},b)\in\mathsf{el}{(\mathcal{G})}}P(\mathcal{C}).

However, describing a similar model for circuit algebras is more challenging. The presence of unconnected graphs means that, neither the method of
[
24
]
, nor that of
[
41
, Corollary 8.14]
may be directly extended to a model structure on
\mathsf{psh_{\mathsf{sSet}}}({\Xi^{\times}})
(or some subcategory thereof) that gives a good description of weak circuit algebras. It is for later work to investigate the existence of a such a model.
